Her resident advisor and an art professor eventually noticed her marked weight loss, fatigue, and withdrawal from the Trailblazers Club and encouraged her to see campus health; after being told she was underweight and at risk of needing to withdraw for medical reasons, she reluctantly agreed to a psychiatric evaluation because she was terrified of losing her academic progress and future career plans.\n- Chief Complaint: I feel like if I don’t keep really tight control over what I eat, I’ll gain weight and everything I’ve worked for will fall apart.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S043 (Restrictive_Intake): Most days I’ll have black coffee in the morning, maybe half a granola bar between classes if I feel faint, and then just pick at a tiny salad in my dorm at night, and I keep a spreadsheet on my laptop to make sure I stay under this strict calorie number I’ve set for myself no matter what else is going on.\n- S044 (Low_Weight): I know everyone keeps saying I’ve gotten “so thin,” but when I look in the mirror I still see places that look soft to me, so I keep thinking I should lose just a little more before I can relax, even though my clothes are hanging off me and I’m always cold in class.\n- S045 (Fear_of_Weight_Gain): If I even imagine eating like a normal college student—pizza at a study group or late-night ice cream with my roommates—I get this panicky feeling that I’ll blow up overnight and all the work I’ve put into being disciplined and successful here will disappear and people will see me as lazy and out of control.\n- S046 (Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance): On days when the scale is lower, I feel like I’m finally doing something right and I’m a bit more worthy of being in the honors program and becoming an art therapist, but if it goes up even half a pound I feel disgusting and like I don’t deserve to be in any of my roles on campus.\n- S084 (Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition): When the campus doctor talked about my heart and bone health and mentioned maybe taking a medical leave, it honestly felt blown way out of proportion to me, because I walk to all my classes, I’m still getting A’s, and apart from being tired and lightheaded sometimes, I don’t feel sick enough for it to be this serious.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: I’ve started skipping club meetings and turning down group projects or dinners because they’d mess up my food schedule, and some days I’m so drained and dizzy that it’s hard to focus in studio and I fall behind on my art pieces even though I’m still clinging to my grades.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. But if it goes up by even half a pound, I feel disgusting and completely unworthy of any of my roles on campus.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any changes in your menstrual cycle, such as irregular periods or a complete stop, since you started tightly controlling your eating and exercising?\n\nPatient: I haven't really been paying much attention to that, to be honest. I've been so stressed with my classes and my art portfolio that I'm not really sure if my periods have changed or not.\n\nDoctor: Let's take a closer look at your menstrual cycle; can you try to recall the last time you had a period, and if you've noticed any changes in how often you get your period since you started your strict diet and exercise regimen?\n\nPatient: I honestly can't recall the last time I had it, and I'm not sure if the timing has changed. I've just been so completely focused on my classes and keeping up with my daily routine that I haven't paid any attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Can you try to think back to the last time you had a period, or at least estimate how long it's been since your last period, as this information is important for understanding your overall health?\n\nPatient: I honestly can't even guess because I haven't kept track of it at all. My mind has been completely consumed by my studio art critiques and keeping up my grades, so I'm really not sure when it last happened. It's just not something I've been paying attention to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any menstrual period in the past three months, as this will help clarify the impact of your eating habits on your reproductive health?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure, because I haven't been keeping track of it at all lately. I've been so overwhelmed with my studio art projects and academic stress that I just haven't paid any attention to it.\n\nDoctor: To clarify, you're unsure if you've had a menstrual period in the past three months, but can you confirm if you've had any periods at all in the past year, as this will help us understand the potential effects of your diet and stress on your menstrual cycle?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure about the exact timing of my periods over the past year because I haven't paid close attention to it. I've been so completely focused on my classes and keeping up with my daily food routines that it just wasn't on my radar.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any menstrual periods at all in the past year, as this information is crucial for understanding the potential impact of your eating habits and stress on your reproductive health?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure, and I don't want to guess and give you the wrong information. I've just been so completely focused on my classes and keeping up with my daily routines that I haven't paid any attention to it at all this past year.\n\nDoctor: To confirm, you're saying you can't recall having a menstrual period in the past year, is that correct?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's correct. I honestly just haven't kept track of it at all, so I really can't say for sure one way or the other.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if you have noticed any other physical symptoms such as hair loss, cold intolerance, or fatigue, which might be related to your eating habits and potential hormonal changes?\n\nPatient: I do get really cold in class sometimes and feel tired or lightheaded, but I haven't noticed any hair loss. Honestly, I don't think it's a major medical issue or that I'm sick enough for it to be serious, since I'm still walking to all my classes and keeping my grades up.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how often you feel tired or lightheaded, and if these feelings are related to your meals or activities, as this might help us understand how your eating habits are affecting your overall health?\n\nPatient: I usually feel dizzy or tired between my classes or when I'm working for hours in the art studio, and I'll sometimes eat half a granola bar if I feel faint just to keep going. But I really don't think it's serious enough to warrant medical leave like they said. I'm still pushing through every day and keeping my grades up, so it's not like I'm too sick to be here.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you can't recall having a menstrual period in the past year, can you confirm if you've experienced any other symptoms like breast tenderness, mood swings, or any vaginal discharge, which could help us better understand your condition?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any of those symptoms, and I'm not sure about mood swings because I'm just generally stressed about my classes. Honestly, the only physical things I've really felt are just being constantly cold and tired.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate your current weight and tell me what you believe would be a 'healthy' or 'acceptable' weight for yourself, as this might help us understand your motivations and fears around eating and weight gain?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure of the exact numbers right now, and I don't really want to focus on a specific weight. I just feel like my current weight is still too high, and I need to keep losing more before I can feel safe and in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your weight and body shape are directly tied to your self-worth and identity as a student and future art therapist?"

Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.
